### that is:
## THE ORDER
## OF ECCLESIASTICAL CHANTING

### found in the church books,
### with the addition of
## The Gospel Pillars[^2]
### according to key words,

### In the Press of the Ruthenian Cathedral Chapter[^3]
### 1852.

# PART I: GENERAL ORDO & DIVINE SERVICES (Principles of Worship and Sacerdotal Rubrics)

The Church Rule (*Пра́вило церко́вное*), or Divine Worship (*Богослуже́нїе*), arranged by the holy Fathers and chanted in church or read in private by persons of the spiritual rank[^5], consists of:
* Vespers (*Вече́рня*)
* Compline (*Павече́рїе*)
* Midnight Office (*Полно́щница*)
* Matins (*Оу҆́треня*)
* First Hour (*Ча́съ а̄-й*)
* Third Hour (*Ча́съ г҃-й*)
* Sixth Hour (*Ча́съ ѕ҃-й*)
* Ninth Hour (*Ча́съ ѳ҃-й*)

After the Sixth Hour, the Divine Liturgy takes place. But if the Divine Liturgy is not celebrated, there is joined to the Sixth Hour, as a representation of the Liturgy that would have taken place, the Typika (*Оу҆бѣ́дница*), or the Order of the Typika (*Послѣ́дованїе изобрази́тельныхъ*)[^6].

> **Remark.** For the celebration of every ecclesiastical rule, the following liturgical books are required[^7]:  
> 1. **Horologion** (*Часосло́въ* / *Молитвосло́въ* / Book of the Hours).  
> 2. **Octoechos** (*Октѡ́ихъ* / Book of Eight Tones), containing the daily services arranged according to the eight tones, that is: for Sunday, the Resurrectional service; for Monday, the Penitential service and that to the Angels; for Tuesday, the service to St. John the Baptist; for Wednesday and Friday, the service to the Precious Cross; for Thursday, to the Holy Apostles and to our holy Father Nicholas; and for Saturday, containing the service to All Saints and for the departed; used from the Second Sunday after the Descent of the Holy Spirit.  
> 3. **Menaion** (*Минї́а*) or **Anthologion** (*Трїфоло́гїонъ* / Festal Menaion), where the services of the fixed Feasts and celebrated Saints are found, as well as the Common Services to Saints (*Ѡ́бщая Минї́а*, *Ѡ҆бщи́нникъ* / General Menaion);  
> 4. **Lenten Triodion** (*Трїѡ́дь по́стная*), beginning from the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee; and **Flowery Triodion** (*Трїѡ́дь цвѣ́тная* / Pentecostarion), used from Saturday before Palm Sunday; wherein are set the services for the movable Feasts;  
> 5. **Psalter** (*Псалти́рь*), divided into 20 Kathismata or sections, and containing at the end the Canticles read in Lent and the Megalynaria;  
> 6. **Heirmologion** (*І҆рмоло́гїонъ*);  
> 7. **Liturgikon** (*Литургіко́нъ* / *Служе́бникъ*), the **Apostolos** (*А҆по́столъ*), and the **Gospel Book** (*Єv́а́ггелїе*).

*(During the Holy Forty Days of the Great Fast, the Typika is joined in part to the Ninth Hour and in part to Vespers).*

## On Vespers[^8]

Vespers is threefold: **Small**, **Weekly (Daily)**, and **Great**.

Great Vespers occurs both with Litiya and without (apart from) Litiya. (The All-Night Vigil comprises Great Vespers with Litiya, Matins with Polyeleos, and the First Hour).

Small Vespers is celebrated (usually in monasteries) according to the Typikon in the evening before an impending All-Night Vigil, before the setting of the sun.

Great Vespers with Litiya (the All-Night Vigil) is celebrated according to the Typikon after the setting of the sun; but according to local custom here, on that very day before the rising of the sun upon which falls whatever Feast of the Lord (Dominical), of the Mother of God, or of a celebrated Saint to whom an All-Night Vigil is rendered[^9].

Great Vespers without Litiya takes place on Saturday evening (facing Sunday), and also on a weekday if on the following day there occurs a Saint having a Polyeleos (without an All-Night Vigil), marked in the Horologion or Anthologion with the sign of the cross ☩[^10].

Weekly or Daily Vespers (middling) is celebrated when on the following weekday—namely: on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, or Saturday—there is a service of one or two common (minor) Saints.

## On Compline

Compline is Small and Great. Small Compline is read throughout the entire year; Great Compline only in the Holy Forty Days of the Great Fast, that is: on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, and Thursday. Likewise, Great Compline is chanted with Litiya on the Feasts of the Nativity of Christ, of the Theophany of the Lord, and of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God, even if it falls during the Fast.

## On the Midnight Office

The Midnight Office is either Daily, or Saturday, or Sunday. Daily Midnight Office is read on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday. Saturday Midnight Office is read only on Saturday; Sunday Midnight Office on the Sundays of the entire year on which no Vigil occurs.

## On Matins

Matins is divided according to the Typikon into Weekly (Daily) and Great. Weekly Matins is celebrated if there is a service of one or two common Saints on weekdays. Great Matins takes place on Sunday (Resurrectional Matins), and likewise on weekdays if there occurs a Saint having an All-Night Vigil or to whom only a Polyeleos is assigned at Matins.

## On the Hours

The First Hour according to the Typikon is always joined to Matins; the Third and Sixth are read together; and the Ninth usually before Vespers in sequence, as is set forth in the Horologion (*Молитвосло́въ* / *Часосло́въ*). Concerning the troparia and kontakia read at the Hours, it is indicated below in their proper places. On the Paramony (Eve) of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany, and likewise on Great Friday, the Royal Hours (*Часы̀ ца́рстїи*) are read.

## On the Divine Liturgy and Typika

The Divine Liturgy in the Eastern Church is threefold, namely: the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom, of St. Basil the Great, and of St. Gregory the Dialogist (the Presanctified)[^11].

The Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom is chanted throughout the entire year, except for those days on which the Typikon forbids it, or on which it commands to chant the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great or the Presanctified.

The Liturgy of St. Basil is chanted without Vespers on the Feast of St. Basil the Great on the 1st of January, and also on the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Sundays of the Holy Great Fast. Together with Vespers, the Liturgy of St. Basil is chanted on the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany, if the Paramony falls on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday. For if the Paramony of both these Feasts falls on Saturday or Sunday, then on the Paramony the Liturgy of Chrysostom takes place, and on the Feast itself of the Nativity or Theophany of the Lord the Liturgy of St. Basil is celebrated without Vespers. Likewise on Great Thursday and on Holy Great Saturday, the Liturgy of St. Basil is chanted together with Vespers.

*Note.* The Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom is also celebrated together with Vespers on the Feast of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God, if it falls on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday of the Holy Forty Days of the Great Fast.

The Liturgy of the Presanctified (Holy) Gifts is celebrated in the Great Fast on Wednesday and Friday, and in Passion Week on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday.

Concerning the Typika (*Оу҆бѣ́дница*) or the Service of the Typika (*И҆зобрази́тельная*), it is fitting to know that it takes place on Sundays, feasts, and other days whenever for any necessity there is no Divine Liturgy. It is read after the Sixth Hour.

# ORDER OF HIERATICAL CELEBRATION,
### that is: how the Deacon serves with the Priest,
### or the Priest alone at Great Vespers,
### Matins, and the Divine Liturgy.

## ORDER
## Of Small Vespers

If an All-Night Vigil is to be celebrated, it is preceded on the Paramony of a Feast of the Lord (except the Nativity and Theophany, wherein Litiya takes place with Compline), of the Mother of God, or of a celebrated Saint to whom a Vigil is assigned, before the setting of the sun, after the striking of the klepalo[^12] on the small bell, by Small Vespers, which is celebrated in this manner:

The Priest, putting on the epitrachelion, makes (according to the Typikon in the narthex, but elsewhere before the Royal Doors)[^13] the usual beginning, that is: **"Blessed is our God..."** (*Благословенъ Богъ нашъ*).  
And the Reader or Choir says: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee."** (*Слава тебѣ Боже нашъ, слава тебѣ*).  
Then: **"O Heavenly King..."** (*Царю небесный*).  
**"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
**"All-Holy Trinity..."** (*Пресвятая Тройце*).  
**"Lord, have mercy"** (three times).  
And after **"Our Father..."** (*Отче нашъ*):  
The Exclamation by the Priest: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."** (*Яко твое есть царство*).  
**"Lord, have mercy"** (twelve times).  
**"Come, let us worship..."** (*Прїидите поклонимся*) (three times).  
*(Thus far the usual beginning).*  
And the Ninth Hour is read in sequence. Then at its conclusion, and after the dismissal, they enter the temple. But if the Ninth Hour is said in the temple, then no dismissal takes place, but immediately the Priest begins: **"Blessed is our God..."** (*Благословенъ Богъ нашъ*).
And the Reader says: **"Come, let us worship..."** (*Прїидите поклонимся*) (three times).  
And the evening psalm: **"Bless the Lord, O my soul"** (*Благослови душе моя Господа*), in a low voice.  
After the psalm: **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
**"Alleluia"** (three times) without singing.  
The Priest does not say the Litany, but the Reader says: **"Lord, have mercy"** (twelve times).  
**"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**  
And immediately: **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*).  
And we set 4 stichera, starting from the verse: **"From the morning watch..."** (*От стражи утреннїя*)[^14].  
At the conclusion of the stichera with **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**  
The Priest exclaims: **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"** (*Премудрость, прости*).  
> **Choir:** **"Gladsome Light"** (*Свѣте тихій*).
> **Priest:** **"Let us attend. Peace be unto all. Wisdom! Let us attend!"**
> **Choir:** "The Daily Prokimenon."
After this: **"Vouchsafe, O Lord, in this evening..."** (*Сподоби Господи въ вечеръ сей*).  
The Litany is not chanted, but immediately we chant the Stichera on the Aposticha (*стїхи́ры на стїхо́внахъ*).  
Then: **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Нынѣ отпущаеши*).  
Trisagion. And after **"Our Father..."**:  
The Troparion of the feast, or the Troparion to the Saint; **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"** Theotokion.  
After this, the Priest says the Litany: **"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy..."** (*Помилуй насъ Боже по велицѣй милости*).  
> **Choir:** **"Lord, have mercy"** (three times), and so forth.
Then: **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God..."** (*Слава тебѣ Христе Боже*), and the Small Dismissal.  
*(For the conclusion of the dismissal, see under the date of the impending feast in the Horologion).*

## ORDER
## Of the All-Night Vigil,
### that is: Great Vespers with Litiya, and Matins with the First Hour,
### on Feasts of the Lord and of the Mother of God,
### and of Celebrated Saints.

After the setting of the sun, or according to local custom here at the 5th hour after midnight before the rising of the sun[^15], the lamplighter goes and strikes the great bell slowly, reciting Psalm 50. And then, having entered, he kindles the lamps and prepares the censer; and going out again, he rings all the bells (*во вся звоны* / festal peal). Returning into the church, he kindles the candles on the candlestick before the Royal Doors, and also upon the Holy Table.

The Priest, having made three bows before the Holy Doors and to both choirs, enters the holy sanctuary, puts on the epitrachelion, kissing the cross upon it (in cathedral and parish churches, the Priest performs this vested in the phelonion, and the Deacon in the sticharion); and having put incense with prayer into the censer, he himself censes the Holy Table crosswise all around and the entire table of oblation, and opens the Holy Doors.

While he stands before the Holy Table, the serving Deacon (or the lamplighter if there is no Deacon), taking a candlestick, precedes the Priest through the north door, and standing in the midst of the church with the lighted candle, exclaims in a loud voice: **"Command, Master!"** (*Повели́те*)[^16].

All having stood up, the Priest signs a cross with the censer three times before the Holy Table; and then he exclaims thus:

**"Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**

And he begins in a low and quiet voice:  
**"Come, let us worship God our King."**  
Then a second time, a little louder:  
**"Come, let us worship and fall down before Christ, our King and God."**  
Again a third time, in a higher voice:  
**"Come, let us worship and fall down before the Very Christ, our King and our God."**

And immediately the Introductory Psalm 103 is read: **"Bless the Lord, O my soul"** (*Благослови душе моя Господа*)[^17]; (elsewhere they chant it slowly, with sweet-singing and with the refrain: **"Blessed art Thou, O Lord,"** the rest of the brethren joining in singing).

The Priest, having gone out through the Holy Doors, makes a cross with the censer before the Holy Doors, moving it vertically and horizontally, and goes and censes in order the holy local icons, first on the right side, then on the left. After this he censes the superior, both choirs according to their rank, and the standing people, and makes a complete circuit with the censer throughout the church, the Deacon or sacristan preceding him with a candle. Whenever the Priest makes a cross with the censer, bowing slightly, the Deacon or candle-bearer also bows with him. Returning again to the Royal Doors, he makes a cross with the censer before the Royal Doors, censes slightly the icon of Christ the Saviour and of the Mother of God, and having entered into the holy sanctuary, he closes the Holy Doors.

When they begin to sing: **"In wisdom hast Thou made them all"** (*Вся премудростїю сотворилъ еси*), the Priest comes before the Royal Doors in epitrachelion only, and standing, recites the Prayers of Light (Vesper Prayers).

The psalm having ended, and the Choir having chanted: **"Alleluia"** (three times), **"Glory to Thee, O God,"** standing in that same place before the Holy Doors (or the Deacon, if there is one, standing in his place on the ambo), he says the Great Litany: **"In peace let us pray to the Lord."**

After the exclamation, they chant selected verses from the First Kathisma of the Psalter: **"Blessed is the man..."** (*Блаженъ мужъ*)[^18], and after them follows the Little Litany by the Deacon or Priest.

After the exclamation of this, the Choir chants **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) in the appointed Tone, the first stichera of the feast; and then the Deacon, having taken a blessing from the Priest (or the Priest himself where there is no Deacon), censes the Holy Table and the entire sanctuary, and going out through the north side, censes the whole church.

At the conclusion of the festal stichera, when we say the verse: **"For His mercy is confirmed upon us..."** (*Яко утвердися милость его на насъ*), both choirs together, then the Priest, having bowed to the superior and entered into the holy sanctuary, vests in the phelonion. And while the Choir sings in the midst of the church **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**, he opens the Royal Doors and makes the Entrance with the censer. And if there is a Deacon, he precedes him with the censer, and they go out (round about the Holy Table) by the north door, two candle-bearers preceding them with candles.

Having arrived before the Royal Doors, they bow toward the altar. The Priest stands directly facing the Holy Doors, and the Deacon stands at the Priest's right hand before the Holy Doors; and bending down, holding his orarion with three fingers of his right hand, he says secretly so that only the Priest hears: **"Let us pray to the Lord."**  
And the Priest says secretly the prayer: **"In the evening and in the morning and at noonday..."**[^19]  
And after the prayer, standing upright, the Deacon says to the Priest, pointing at the same time toward the east, holding the orarion with three fingers of his right hand: **"Bless, Master, the holy entrance."**  
And the Priest blesses toward the east, saying: **"Blessed is the entrance of Thy holy ones, O Lord, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
Then the Deacon turns toward the Royal Doors, and standing before their threshold, awaits the completion of the sticheron. (If a Bishop happens to be in the church, the Deacon goes to him, censes him, and bowing his head before him, receives a blessing, the Priest doing none of these things).

Upon the completion of the sticheron, the Deacon enters into the midst, and tracing a cross with the censer, exclaims in a loud voice: **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"** (*Премудрость, прости*).  
*(If there is no Deacon, the Priest himself does this).*  
And the Choir sings: **"Gladsome Light of the holy glory..."** (*Свѣте тихій святыя славы*).  
The Deacon (or if there is none, the Priest himself), having entered into the holy sanctuary, censes the Holy Table.

The Priest, having bowed before the Holy Doors and kissed them, enters into the holy sanctuary.

At the conclusion of **"Gladsome Light,"** the Deacon says: **"Let us attend."**  
> **Priest:** **"Peace be unto all."**
And again the Deacon: **"Wisdom! Let us attend!"**  
And the Prokimenon is chanted, and its verses are read.

The Priest, having turned toward the west with folded hands, stands awaiting the completion of the Prokimenon; then he bows before the Holy Table and stands thus, looking toward the east.

If there are Old Testament Readings (Paroemias), after the Prokimenon the Deacon says (if there is none, the Priest himself): **"Wisdom!"**  
The Reader says whence they are taken, namely: **"The Reading from Genesis"** [or **Proverbs**, or the **Catholic Epistle of [Name]**].  
And again the Deacon: **"Let us attend."**

After the readings, immediately the Deacon, standing in the midst of the church upon the ambo-stone, says the Litany: **"Let us all say..."** (*Рцемъ вси*), and the exclamation is by the Priest.

After the exclamation, the Choir reads: **"Vouchsafe, O Lord, in this evening..."** (*Сподоби Господи въ вечеръ сей*).  
And again the Deacon (or the Priest before the Holy Table, if there is no Deacon): **"Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord."**  
And the exclamation from the Priest: **"For good and a lover of mankind art Thou..."**  
Then: **"Peace be unto all."**  
And the Deacon: **"Let us bow our heads unto the Lord."**  
The Priest exclaims: **"Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy kingdom..."** (*Буди держава*).

After this, the Deacon, taking the censer and asking a blessing, goes out before the Priest, who takes the holy cross; and two candle-bearers preceding them, they go out into the narthex (if there is no Deacon, the Priest himself goes out with the censer and cross). All the brethren and the choirs also go out with them into the narthex, singing the Litiya stichera of the feast (or the first sticheron of the temple patron, and of the Saint if he has a Vigil). And there the Deacon (or the Priest himself if there is no Deacon) censes all the holy icons, the superior, the choirs, and all according to custom, and having put aside the censer, stands in his place.

At the conclusion of the stichera with **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**, the Deacon (or in the absence of a Deacon, the Priest himself) exclaims in the hearing of all this prayer: **"Save, O God, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."** (*Спаси Боже люди твоя, и благослови достоянїе твое*), as in the Horologion, unto the end of the twelve **"Lord, have mercy,"** and the remaining prayers.

After this the Priest exclaims: **"Hear us, O God our Saviour..."** (*Услыши ны Боже*).  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
Then he says: **"Peace be unto all."**  
The Deacon says: **"Let us bow our heads unto the Lord."**  
And all having bowed their heads and bent down to the ground, the Priest prays in a loud voice: **"O most merciful Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God..."** (*Владыко многомилостиве Господи Іисусе Христе Боже нашъ*).

Upon its completion, the Choir chanting **"Amen,"** we enter into the temple, and the Stichera on the Aposticha (*стихо́вныя стихи̑ры*) are sung by both choirs together.

And candlesticks are set on either side of the pre-adorned Tetrapod (*четвероно́жецъ*)[^20], upon which four vessels are placed crosswise for the blessing, thus:
* One dish with five loaves in the middle at the top;
* The second, containing wheat, below facing the first;
* The third vessel, containing wine, on the side of the small choir, that is, on the right side where the icon of the Mother of God is;
* The fourth, containing oil, on the side of the great choir, that is, on the left side where the icon of Christ the Master is[^21].

At the conclusion of the stichera: **"Now lettest Thou..."** (*Нынѣ отпущаеши*).  
Trisagion. And after **"Our Father..."**, the Priest exclaims: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."**  
And the singers chant: if it is a Feast of the Lord or of the Mother of God, the Troparion of the feast three times; but if the memory of a great Saint, the Troparion of the Saint twice, and **"Rejoice, O Virgin Mother of God..."** (*Богородице Дѣво*) once (for the rest, see the rubric).

While the last troparion is sung in a loud voice and slowly (prolongedly), the Deacon, having taken a blessing from the Priest (or according to our custom, the Priest himself), censes the Tetrapod crosswise, upon which the aforesaid gifts are prepared for blessing, and again the loaves from the front only.  
And the Deacon holding the censer with his left hand, and his orarion with three fingers of his right hand, and having said: **"Let us pray to the Lord,"**  
The Priest says the prayer in a loud voice: **"O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless the five loaves and therewith didst satisfy five thousand men..."** (*Господи Іисусе Христе Боже нашъ, благословивый пять хлѣбовъ*)[^22].

And lifting up one loaf, he signs with it the rest of the loaves. And when he says: **"DO THOU THYSELF (AND NOW, O MASTER) BLESS..."** (*САМЪ [и НЫНѢ ВЛАДЫКО] БЛАГОСЛОВИ*), he points with his right hand, having turned it with palm upward, toward the loaves: **"THESE LOAVES..."** (*ХЛѢБЫ СЇЯ*); likewise toward the wheat, wine, and oil: **"THE WHEAT, THE WINE, AND THE OIL."** (*ПШЕНИЦУ, ВИНО И ЕЛЕЙ*).

At the conclusion of the prayer and after the **"Amen,"** the Choir sings: **"Blessed be the name of the Lord, from this time forth and unto all ages"** (three times) in Tone 4.  
Then Psalm 33 is read: **"I will bless the Lord at all times..."** (*Благословлю Господа на всякое время*). And we say it up to: **"They shall not want any good thing."**[^23]
**"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
**"Lord, have mercy"** (three times); **"Father, bless."**  
The Priest, going, stands before the Royal Doors, awaiting the completion of the Psalm, looking toward the west. And upon the completion of the Psalm, the Priest, blessing toward the people, says: **"The blessing of the Lord be upon you, through His grace and love for mankind, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**

The Choir, having said **"Amen,"** begins:  
**"Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men"** (three times).  
Then:  
**"O Lord, Thou wilt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise"** (twice), in a meek and quiet voice.  
After this, he reads the Six Psalms (*Шестопсалмі́е* / *шестопса́лмы*)[^24].  
The Priest divests himself of the sacred vestments. The candles are also extinguished, leaving only the lamp burning before the icon of the Master and of the Mother of God.  
After three psalms: **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
**"Alleluia"** (three times), **"Lord, have mercy"** (three times), **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**  
**"O Lord of my salvation..."** (*Господи спасенїя моего*).  
Then the Priest, having gone out in the epitrachelion, recites the Morning Prayers before the Holy Doors.

Upon the completion of the Six Psalms, after **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**, he says the Litany: **"In peace let us pray to the Lord."**  
Then they begin to sing: **"God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us..."** (*Богъ Господь и явися намъ*).  
And after each chant of the choir, the Priest or the Reader exclaims also its verses.  
After this, the Troparion of the feast, or as is specified in the Typikon; and the Priest, stepping down, stands in his place.  
And immediately the Choir says: **"Lord, have mercy"** (three times).  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:"**  
> **Reader:** **"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:"**
And he recites the stichology of the Kathisma of the Psalter.

Upon the completion of each stichology, the Priest says the Little Litany: **"Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord,"** and the exclamation as in the Horologion.  
The Choir sings the Sessional Hymn (*Сѣда́ленъ*) of the feast (or as indicated), and **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
Then the second Kathisma and the second Sessional Hymn with **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**

And they chant the Polyeleos, that is: the Psalm **"Praise the name of the Lord..."** (*Хвалите имя Господне*)[^25], and the Megalynarion with the selected verses from the Psalms of David.  
After this the Little Litany is said, then the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos.

[It is fitting to know also this: that at the Vigil of the all-night singing during the Polyeleos—that is, when they sing the Psalms **"Praise the name of the Lord"** and **"Give thanks unto the Lord"**—an analogion is set in the midst of the church, and upon it is placed the festal icon of the Day of the Lord, or of the Mother of God, or of the holy man or woman; and all the candles are kindled. And the Priest vests in white sticharion, or all the concelebrating clergy, if there are any, in phelonia according to custom. And having gone forth from the sanctuary through the opened Royal Doors, they stand on both sides around the analogion. And the superior distributes candles to the concelebrants and to the people standing in the holy temple. After the distribution, taking the censer, the Deacon preceding him with a burning candle, he censes first the icon upon the analogion round about, and goes to the altar and there censes the Holy Table and the entire sanctuary, and in the temple all the holy local icons; then the concelebrating clergy on both sides and both choirs...][^26]

Thereupon, passing through, he censes all the standing people, and again the holy doors and only two icons, of Christ and of the Mother of God, and that upon the analogion. At that same time they sing the Megalynarion, the Priest beginning it first together with the concelebrating clergy. After this customary chanting, the Little Litany is said by the Deacon, and the Sessional Hymns are chanted. And all the concelebrating clergy enter into the holy sanctuary and divest themselves of the sacred vestments; only the Priest holding the turn remains vested, for the reading of the Gospel.][^27]

After this, the Gradual Hymns (*Степе́нна* / *Anavathmoi*): the first Antiphon of Tone 4: **"From my youth up..."** (*Ѿ ю́ности*) (unless a feast of the Mother of God or of a Saint falls on a Sunday, for then the Antiphons of the occurring tone are chanted). During these the Ponomar goes forth and strikes the bells. While the Priest stands vested in the phelonion before the holy table, the Deacon says:  
**"Let us be attentive."** (*Во́нмимъ*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Peace be unto all."** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*)  
> **Deacon:** ""
**"Wisdom, let us be attentive."** (*Премꙋ́дрость, во́нмимъ*)  
The Choir sings the Prokeimenon, and the Reader proclaims the verse.  
Upon the completion of the Prokeimenon, the Deacon (if there is no Deacon, the Priest) says:  
**"Let us pray to the Lord."** (*Го́сподꙋ помо́лимсѧ*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"For holy art Thou, O our God, and Thou restest in the saints, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ ст҃ъ е҆сѝ Бж҃е на́шъ...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
The Reader proclaims:  
**"Let every breath praise the Lord:"** (*Вся́кое дыха́нїе да хва́литъ Го́спода*)  
and the verse. And the Choir sings.  
Then the Deacon says:  
**"And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the holy Gospel, let us pray to the Lord God in peace."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ е́же сподо́битисѧ на́мъ слы́шанїю ст҃а́гѡ Є҆ѵⷢлїа, ми́ромъ Го́спода Бо́га мо́лимъ*)  
> **Choir:** **"Lord, have mercy"** (three times).
> **Priest:** **"Peace be unto all."**
> **Choir:** **"And to thy spirit."**
> **Deacon:** ""
**"Wisdom! Standing upright, let us hear the holy Gospel."** (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти оу҆слы́шимъ ст҃а́гѡ е҆ѵⷢлїа*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"The reading from the holy Gospel according to [Name]."** (*Ѿ [и҆́мѧкъ] е҆ѵⷢлїа чте́нїе*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Glory to Thee, O Lord, glory to Thee."** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀ Го́споди, сла́ва тебѣ̀*)  

> **Deacon:** **"Let us be attentive."** (*Во́нмимъ*)
And the Priest reads the Morning Gospel of the feast (if the celebrated Saint falls on a Sunday, then the Resurrectional Gospel of the cycle is read).  
Then Psalm 50.[^28]  
While this is being recited, the Priest goes forth through the holy doors with the Gospel Book, holding it before his breast, two candle-bearers with candles preceding him on either side; he stands in the midst of the temple holding the holy Gospel Book before his breast (or he places it upon an analogion). And all approach in good order, make two bows, and kiss the holy Gospel Book, and again make one small bow, bowing the head until each reaches the ground with his hand.[^29]  
When Psalm 50 is concluded, they sing:  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:"**  
**"Today all things are filled with joy..."** (*Вся́ческаѧ дне́сь ра́дости*) (if it is a feast of the Lord); or:  
**"Through the prayers of the Apostles, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions."** (*Моли́твами а҆по́столъ...*)  
**"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
The same verse:  
**"Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions."** (*Моли́твами Богоро́дицы...*)  
Then the refrain:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out our iniquities."** (*Поми́лꙋй на́съ, Бо́же...*)  
and the Sticheron, Tone 6.  
After the kissing of the holy Gospel Book, the Priest blesses the brethren with the Gospel Book and enters into the holy sanctuary.  
The Deacon says at the customary place of the ambo (or the Priest himself, if there is no Deacon) the prayer:  
**"O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."** (*Спасѝ Бо́же лю́ди твоя̑...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times).
And the exclamation from the Priest is this:  
**"Through the mercy and compassions and love for mankind of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ми́лостїю и҆ щедро́тами и҆ человѣколю́бїемъ...*)  
And after **"Amen,"** they begin the Heirmos of the Canon; then they read also the troparia up to 14 with the refrains. (The approaching people are signed by the Priest with the holy oil blessed at the All-Night Vigil).[^30]  
— Thereupon the Priest divests himself of the sacred vestments, and having closed the holy doors, stands at his customary place.

The Deacon stands in the sticharion. It is fitting also to extinguish the candles here.  
After the Third Ode, the Little Litany is said by the Deacon, and the exclamation by the Priest within the sanctuary. Then the Sessional Hymn or Hypakoe.  
After the Sixth Ode, again the Little Litany and the Kontakion.  
At the end of the Eighth Ode, when we are about to begin the Ninth Ode, we all make a metania together, saying:  
**"We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages."** (*Хва́лимъ, благослови́мъ, покланя́емсѧ Го́сподеви, пою́ще и҆ превознося́ще е҆го̀ во вся̀ вѣ́ки*)  
Then the Heirmos of the Eighth Ode, the Katavasia.  
The Priest vests according to custom. And upon the completion of the Eighth Ode, the Deacon having gone forth and traced a cross with the censer before the holy doors, exclaims:  
**"The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!"** (*Богоро́дицꙋ и҆ ма́терь свѣ́та въ пѣ́снехъ возвели́чимъ*)  
And they sing the Heirmos of the Ninth Ode of the feast with the refrain (or as indicated).  
The Typikon prescribes for the Priest or Deacon to cense here the holy sanctuary, the whole church, and the brethren.[^31]  
After the Ninth Ode, the Little Litany and the exclamation:  
**"For all the powers of heaven praise Thee, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ тебѐ хва́лѧтъ вся̀ си́лы...*)  
After this, the Exapostilarion (*Свѣти́ленъ*) is read.  
— At the Praises they sing:  
**"Let every breath praise the Lord..."** (*Вся́кое дыха́ніе да хва́литъ Го́спода*)  
in the tone of the first sticheron. Then they read Psalms 148, 149, and 150, and the Stichera at the Praises are chanted according to custom. Then both choirs unite and sing at the convergence: **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
The Ponomar kindles the candles.  
The Priest vests in epitrachelion and phelonion, opens the royal doors, and goes forth from the sanctuary (with the Deacon, if there is one, going forth through the south door), and makes one bow.  
And at the conclusion of the sticheron, the Priest, having bowed toward the east, exclaims:

**"Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light!"** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀, показа́вшемꙋ на́мъ свѣ́тъ*)  
The Choir with those present sings the Great Doxology, that is:  
**"Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men..."** (*Сла́ва въ вы́шнихъ Бо́гꙋ...*)  
After the Doxology, when they finish singing **"Holy God..."** (*Святы́й Бо́же*), the Priest bows and enters into the holy sanctuary; and the Deacon, after the singing of the Troparion, standing at the customary place of the ambo, or the Priest before the holy table, says the Litany:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee, hear and have mercy."** (*Поми́лꙋй на́съ, Бо́же, по вели́цѣй ми́лости...*)  
and the exclamation:  
**"For Thou art a merciful God, and lovest mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ ми́лостивъ и҆ человѣколю́бецъ...*)  
After this immediately:  
**"Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord."** (*И҆спо́лнимъ оу҆́треннюю моли́твꙋ на́шꙋ...*)  
and after the exclamation the Priest says:  
**"Peace be unto all."** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*)  
> **Choir:** **"And to thy spirit."** (*И҆ дꙋ́хови твое́мꙋ*)
> **Deacon:** **"Let us bow our heads unto the Lord."** (*Главы̑ ва́ша Го́сподеви приклони́те*)
The Priest reads the prayer secretly, then the exclamation:  
**"For Thine it is to show mercy and to save us, O our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Твоѐ бо е́сть е́же ми́ловати...*)  
Thereupon the Deacon: **"Wisdom!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость*)  
> **Choir:** **"Bless!"** (*Благословѝ*)
The Priest, standing according to the Typikon in the royal doors, says:  
**"Blessed is Christ our God, Who is, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Сы́й благослове́нъ Христо́съ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Confirm, O God, the holy Orthodox Christian faith, unto ages of ages!"** (*Оу҆твердѝ, Бо́же, правосла́внꙋю вѣ́рꙋ Хрїстїа́нскꙋю во вѣ́къ вѣ́ка*)[^32]  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Most holy Theotokos, save us!"** (*Пресвята́я Богоро́дице, спасѝ на́съ*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"More honorable than the Cherubim, and more glorious beyond compare than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, true Theotokos, thee do we magnify."** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю херꙋвї́мъ...*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!"** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀, Хрїстѐ Бо́же, оу҆пова́ніе на́ше, сла́ва тебѣ̀*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Lord, have mercy"** (three times). **"Bless!"**  
Then the Priest makes the Dismissal of the occurring feast or saint — see the direction and the Horologion.[^33]  
After this, the royal doors are closed.  
The Choir immediately: **"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times), and the First Hour is read; and at the end the Priest says:  
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ our God, our hope, glory to Thee!"**  
and the complete Dismissal.

## The Order
### OF GREAT VESPERS,
#### Chanted on Saturday Evening for Sunday.

The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, and standing before the royal doors, says:  
**"Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen. Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... Trisagion... Most Holy Trinity... Our Father..."**
The Priest, exclamation:  
**"For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** **"Amen. Lord, have mercy"** (12 times). **"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times).
And Psalm 103: **"Bless the Lord, O my soul: O Lord my God, Thou art become exceedingly great..."**†)[^34]  
The Priest, standing before the holy doors, says the Prayers of Light [Lucernarium Prayers].[^35]  
Upon the completion of the Introductory Psalm, the Choir sings:  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia: glory to Thee, O God"** (three times, with chanting).  
The Priest says the Litany:  
**"In peace let us pray to the Lord..."**  
before the royal doors. (If there is a Deacon, he vests here in the sticharion and, having gone forth by the north door,

[^36] †) *If the Ninth Hour on Saturday was not said together with the Third and Sixth, but is read here before Vespers, then after the customary beginning the Psalms of the Ninth Hour are read. After them no Dismissal takes place (unless it was read in the narthex), but the Priest says the beginning:* **"Blessed is our God..."** *and the Choir immediately:* **"Come, let us worship..."** (three times) *and Psalm 103:* **"Bless the Lord, O my soul..."**

and having stood at the customary place of the ambo, says the Litany.)  
After the exclamation of the Priest, the Choir sings the First Kathisma of the Psalter:  
**"Blessed is the man..."** (*Блаже́нъ мꙋ́жъ*)[^37]  
that is, selected verses of the First Antiphon with "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages"; and the second and third Antiphons of the Kathisma are read by the Reader.  
After the Kathisma, the Priest (or Deacon) says the Little Litany:  
**"Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord..."**  
and the exclamation:  
**"For Thine is the dominion, and Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ твоя̀ держа́ва...*)  
Then they sing:  
**"Lord, I have cried unto Thee, hear me..."** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*)  
in the occurring tone of the Octoechos cycle, and then the Priest, having entered into the sanctuary, puts incense into the censer and says the Prayer of Incense secretly, and thus censes (or the Deacon censes) the holy table crosswise round about and the entire Prothesis, and having gone out through the north door, goes and censes the holy icons: on the south side the icon of Christ the Savior and of the patron of the temple and the others on that side; likewise on the left side the icon of the Theotokos and the others, then the Superior; and thus he censes the entire right choir and the left choir, raising the censer before each straight and across, making a cross, and bows to them in good order, to all equally, and they all likewise make a bow to him; he censes also all the brethren, first directly toward the doors, then on the right and on the left side; then having bowed slightly he returns to the royal doors, and having made the sign of the cross before the royal doors, enters into the holy sanctuary through the south door, and makes the sign of the cross before the holy table and puts away the censer.[^38]  
The Choir, having read through the remaining verses of Psalm 140 and Psalm 141, set—if it is Sunday—ten verses, beginning from:
**"Bring my soul out of prison, that I may praise Thy name."** (*И҆зведѝ и҆зъ те́мницы дꙋ́шꙋ мою̀*)  
and the Stichera written for **"Lord, I have cried"** are sung, as the rubrical table inscribed hereafter indicates. And when the verse is said:  
**"For His mercy is confirmed upon us, and the truth of the Lord endureth forever."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ оу҆тверди́сѧ ми́лость е҆гѡ̀ на на́съ*)  
then the Priest, having made a bow, enters into the holy sanctuary and vests in the phelonion. When they begin to sing  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**  
in the midst of the church, the Priest opens the royal doors, and going around the holy table by the north side, makes the Entrance with the censer, having his phelonion let down. (If there is a Deacon, he does everything here that is done at the All-Night Vigil.) Two servers with candles precede him. And having stood before the holy doors, he says secretly the Entrance Prayer, and awaits the completion of the First Theotokion (Dogmatikon).[^39]  
— Thereupon he traces a cross with the censer before the holy doors, and exclaims:  
**"Wisdom! Standing upright!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти*)  
The Choir with those present sings:  
**"Gladsome Light of the holy glory of the immortal, heavenly, holy, blessed Father, O Jesus Christ..."** (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*).  
And the Priest, having entered into the sanctuary, censes crosswise round about the holy table.  
At the conclusion of the chanting, the Priest or Deacon exclaims:  
**"Let us be attentive! Peace be unto all! Wisdom, let us be attentive!"**  
The Choir sings the Prokeimenon of the day:  
**"The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty."** (*Госпо́дь воцари́сѧ, въ лѣ́потꙋ сѧ ѡ҆блече́*)  
and the Reader reads also its verses. The Priest, having turned toward the west with hands folded, stands awaiting the completion of the Prokeimenon; then he bows toward the east, and having stood before the holy table (or the Deacon standing at the customary place), says the Litany:
**"Let us all say with our whole soul and with our whole mind, let us say:"** (*Рце́мъ всѝ ѿ всея̀ дꙋшѝ...*)  
After the exclamation of the Priest, the Choir reads:  
**"Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin..."** (*Сподо́би, Го́споди, въ ве́черъ се́й...*)  
and thereafter the Priest or Deacon says:  
**"Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord."** (*И҆спо́лнимъ вече́рнюю моли́твꙋ на́шꙋ...*)  
and the exclamation from the Priest:  
**"For Thou art a good God and lovest mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ бл҃гъ и҆ ми́лостивъ...*)  
**"Peace be unto all."**  
> **Choir:** **"And to thy spirit."**
> **Deacon:** **"Let us bow our heads unto the Lord."**
The Priest reads the prayer secretly, and again the exclamation:  
**"Blessed and glorified be the dominion of Thy kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Бꙋ́ди держа́ва ца́рствїя твоегѡ̀...*)  
Then they begin to sing the Aposticha [Stichera on the Verses], **"Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages,"** Theotokion.  
— After this:  
**"Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word..."** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*)  
Trisagion; and after **"Our Father,"** the exclamation from the Priest:  
**"For Thine is the kingdom..."** in the sanctuary.  
The Choir sings the Troparia according to custom, as the Typikon indicates. And upon the completion of the Troparia with "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," the Great Dismissal takes place, that is:  
The Priest (or the Deacon having stepped up to the royal doors) exclaims:  
**"Wisdom!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость*)  
> **Choir:** **"Bless!"** (*Благословѝ*)
> **Priest:** ""
**"Blessed and glorified is Christ our God, Who is, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Сы́й благослове́нъ и҆ препросла́вленъ Христо́съ Бо́гъ на́шъ*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Confirm, O God, the Catholic faith, unto ages of ages!"** (*Оу҆твердѝ, Бо́же, вѣ́рꙋ каѳоли́ческꙋю во вѣ́къ вѣ́ка*)[^40]  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Most holy Theotokos, save us!"** (*Пресвята́я Богоро́дице, спасѝ на́съ*)  
> **Choir:** **"More honorable than the Cherubim..."**
> **Priest:** ""
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!"** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀, Хрїстѐ Бо́же, оу҆пова́нїе на́ше, сла́ва тебѣ̀*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Lord, have mercy"** (three times). **"Bless!"**  
The Priest, standing according to the Typikon in the royal doors facing the people, says the Dismissal:  
**"May Christ our true God, Who rose from the dead..."** (*Воскресы́й и҆зъ ме́ртвыхъ Христо́съ и҆́стинный Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
and the rest, as in the Horologion, and commemorates the saint whose service is sung.*)[^41]

*) *If Vespers on Saturday evening is read outside the church, in private [in cella], then at "Lord, I have cried" and at the Aposticha they read the Common Stichera, and those which are found in the Horologion in the Sunday service of Tone 6.*

> **Remark.** In this manner, as is here delineated for Saturday evening, Vespers with the Entrance is celebrated on a weekday if there occurs a Saint having a Polyeleos, only that after the daily Prokeimenon there are three readings [Paroemias / Old Testament Lessons], and then the Priest before each reading exclaims:  
> **"Wisdom!"**  
> **Reader:** ""
> **"The reading from the Prophecy of [Name];"** or **"The reading from the Proverbs of Solomon;"** or **"The reading from the General Epistle of [Name]."**  
> And again the Priest: **"Let us be attentive!"**  
> and the Reader reads the Paroemia. The Priest or Deacon says the Litany:  
> **"Let us all say with our whole soul, let us say..."**  
> and all the rest as was depicted a little above for Saturday Great Vespers unto the end, and in the Dismissal he commemorates the celebrated saint.[^42]

**Behold.** On Saturday evening Small Compline is read, whose order is delineated below after Daily Vespers.

## The Order
### OF SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE

Sunday Midnight Office is celebrated before the royal doors in the church.  
The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, makes the beginning:  
**"Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
And immediately the Choir says:  
**"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... Trisagion... Most Holy Trinity... Our Father... For Thine is the kingdom... Lord, have mercy"** (12 times). **"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times), and Psalm 50.  
Then the Triadic Canon of the tone in the Octoechos is chanted.[^43]  
After the Canon, they sing the Triadic Megalynarion, that is:  
**"It is truly meet in godlike manner to glorify Thee, O Holy Trinity..."** (*Досто́йнѡ е́сть бг҃оподо́бнѣ сла́вити тя̀ Тр҃це ст҃ая*)  
as in the Horologion, and the other Troparia, and  
**"It is truly meet..."** (*Досто́йнѡ е́сть ꙗ҆́кѡ вои́стиннꙋ*).  
Thereupon Trisagion; and after "Our Father," the exclamation.  
Then among us [in our Ruthenian practice] the Troparia:  
**"Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us, for laying aside all excuse, we sinners offer this supplication unto Thee as Master: have mercy on us."** (*Поми́лꙋй на́съ Го́споди, поми́лꙋй на́съ...*)  
and the Litany:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy..."**  
**"Again we pray that this city (or: this village) may be preserved..."**  
**"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times);  
and the exclamation:  
**"Hear us, O God our Savior, the hope of all the ends of the earth..."** (*Оу҆слы́ши ны̀ Бо́же...*)  
and the rest, as in the Horologion at the end of Daily Midnight Office, and the Dismissal.  
Then the Priest receives the customary mutual forgiveness and says:  
**"Let us pray for the most holy Universal Pontiff [Pope of Rome]..."** (*Помо́лимсѧ ѡ҆ ст҃ѣ́йшемъ вселе́нскомъ А҆рхїере́и...*)[^44]  
and the rest.

*) *If Sunday Midnight Office is read in private [in cella], one reads the beautiful Triadic Canon written in the Horologion, and at the end after the Troparia and after "Our Father": "Lord, have mercy" (40 times); then the Prayer:* **"O Almighty and Life-creating Trinity..."** (*Всемогꙋ́щаѧ и҆ животворя́щаѧ Тр҃це...*)[^45]

## The Order
### OF SUNDAY MATINS,
#### or Resurrectional Matins.

The Priest, after the Dismissal of the Sunday Midnight Office, wearing the epitrachelion only, making a bow, makes the beginning:  
**"Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
And immediately:  
**"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times),  
and the Psalms of King David: [Psalm 19] **"The Lord hear thee..."** (*Оу҆слы́шитъ тя̀ Го́сподь*) and [Psalm 20] **"O Lord, in Thy strength..."** (*Го́споди си́лою твое́ю*).  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Trisagion... Most Holy Trinity... Our Father..."**  
and the exclamation:  
**"For Thine is the kingdom..."**  
Then the Troparia:  
**"O Lord, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting victories to our Emperor over his adversaries, and by Thy Cross preserving Thy commonwealth."** (*Спасѝ Го́споди лю́ди твоя̀...*)  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:"**  
**"Thou Who wast voluntarily lifted up upon the Cross, bestow Thy mercies upon the new commonwealth called by Thy name, O Christ God..."** (*Вознесы́йсѧ на Крⷭ҇тъ...*)  
**"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**  
**"O champion and dread Protectress, despise not our prayers, O Good One..."** (*Предста́тельнице стра́шнаѧ...*)  
After these the Priest says the Litany for the Emperor [Ца́рская екте́нїя]:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy..."**  
and:  
**"Again we pray for our most pious Emperor [Franz Joseph I]..."**  
and the rest; and after it the Choir answers six times:  
**"Lord, have mercy."**  
After the exclamation, the Choir says:  
**"In the name of the Lord, bless, Father."** (*И҆́менемъ Госпо́днимъ благословѝ, ѻ҆́тче*)  
And the Priest exclaims:  
**"Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Сла́ва святѣ́й, и҆ единосꙋ́щнѣй, и҆ животворя́щѣй, и҆ нераздѣ́льнѣй Тро́йцѣ...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen,"**
and:  
**"Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men"** (three times),  
**"O Lord, Thou wilt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise"** (twice).  
Then

> **Behold.** Whenever the Midnight Office was not celebrated in the church, Matins begins from the customary beginning [i.e., with "Glory to the holy, consubstantial..."][^46].

he reads the Six Psalms, with all attentiveness and fear of God. The Priest withdraws to his place.  
After three psalms, that is: when they begin to read the Psalm:  
**"O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before Thee..."** [Psalm 87]  
the Priest, having gone forth, says the Morning Prayers, standing before the holy doors.  
After the Six Psalms, when the Choir sings  
**"Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia: glory to Thee, O God"** (three times),  
the Priest, standing before the royal doors, says the Great Litany:  
**"In peace let us pray to the Lord."**  
And after the exclamation, the Choir proclaims:  
**"God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us: blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord."** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь и҆ ꙗ҆ви́сѧ на́мъ...*)  
and sings it in the occurring tone of the Octoechos. The Priest or the Reader reads also its verses.  
Then they sing the Resurrectional Troparion twice; "Glory..." of the Saint of that day; "Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages:" Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion of the saint. [Or as is otherwise indicated in the Typikon.]  
After the troparia, the Choir immediately says:  
**"Lord, have mercy"** (three times).  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:"**  
> **Reader:** **"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**
and reads the Kathisma of the Psalter.  
Upon the completion of the stichology, the Priest says the Little Litany:  
**"Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord"** (secretly),  
the Choir sings the litany according to custom, and after this the Resurrectional Sessional Hymn from the Octoechos, first plainly [without verse], then the refrain:  
**"Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, forget not Thy poor unto the end."** (*Воскреснѝ Го́споди Бо́же мо́й...*)  
and the second Sessional Hymn; "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," Theotokion.  
Then follows the second stichology, the Little Litany, and the second Sessional Hymn; "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," and its Theotokion.  
Then the Reader reads the Blameless [Amomos], that is: Kathisma XVII:  
**"Blessed are the blameless in the way, who walk in the law of the Lord."** [Psalm 118]  
(or they sing the Polyeleos if the Typikon prescribes it). During which the Priest censes according to the Typikon.
The Choir immediately sings the Troparia:  
**"The assembly of the Angels was amazed..."** (*А҆́ггелскїй собо́ръ...*)  
with the refrain:  
**"Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes."** (*Благослове́нъ е҆сѝ Го́споди...*)  
After these troparia follows the Little Litany with the exclamation:  
**"For blessed is Thy name, and glorified is Thy kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ благослови́сѧ...*)  
Then the Hypakoe of the tone. And the Gradual Hymns (*Степе́нны* / *Anavathmoi*) of the occurring tone, during which the Ponomar goes forth and strikes the bells.  
After the Antiphons, the Priest vests in epitrachelion and phelonion, opens the holy doors, and exclaims:  
**"Let us be attentive! Peace be unto all! Wisdom, let us be attentive!"**  
(If there is a Deacon, he acts in all things as at the All-Night Vigil, see above).  
The Choir sings the Prokeimenon. Upon the completion of the Prokeimenon, the Priest, standing in the sanctuary, says:  
**"Let us pray to the Lord."**  
> **Choir:** **"Lord, have mercy."**
> **Priest:** ""
**"For holy art Thou, O our God, and Thou restest in the saints, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
The Reader proclaims, and the Choir also sings:  
**"Let every breath praise the Lord."**  
The Reader, verse:  
**"Praise God in His saints, praise Him in the firmament of His power."**  
The Priest exclaims:  
**"And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the holy Gospel, let us pray to the Lord God."**  
> **Choir:** **"Lord, have mercy"** (three times).
> **Priest:** **"Peace be unto all."**
> **Choir:** **"And to thy spirit."**
> **Priest:** ""
**"Wisdom! Standing upright, let us hear the holy Gospel. The reading from the holy Gospel according to [Name]."**  
> **Choir:** **"Glory to Thee, O Lord, glory to Thee."**
> **Priest:** **"Let us be attentive,"**
and reads the Resurrectional (Morning) Gospel of the cycle. Thereafter we say:  
**"Having seen the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship the Holy Lord Jesus, the only Sinless One..."** (*Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во ви́дѣвше...*)  
and Psalm 50.  
While this is being recited, the Priest goes forth through the holy doors with the Gospel Book, holding it before his breast in the midst of the temple for the veneration of the approaching people (or he places it upon the Tetrapod for veneration and leaves it until the Doxology).[^47]
Upon the completion of Psalm 50, the Choir sings:  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:"**  
**"Through the prayers of the Apostles, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions."**  
and:  
**"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**  
**"Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions."**  
Then:  
**"Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression."**  
and the Resurrectional Sticheron in Tone 6:  
**"Jesus having risen from the tomb, as He foretold, hath given us life eternal and great mercy."** (*Воскре́съ І҆исо́усъ ѿ гро́ба...*)  
Then the Priest in the sanctuary (or the Deacon, if there is one, at his place on the ambo) says the prayer:  
**"O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."**  
> **Choir:** **"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times).
and the exclamation:  
**"Through the mercy and compassions, and love for mankind of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
After this, the holy doors are closed, and they begin the Heirmos of the Canon. And they sing the Canons from the Octoechos and to the Saint, as the Typikon indicates. All the troparia of the canons are read up to 14 with the refrains. To the first troparion the refrain is:  
**"Glory to Thy holy Resurrection, O Lord."** (*Сла́ва Го́споди свято́мꙋ воскресе́нїю твоемꙋ̀*)  
To the troparia of the Cross-Resurrectional Canon the refrain is:  
**"Glory to Thy Cross and Resurrection, O Lord."** (*Сла́ва крⷭ҇тꙋ̀ и҆ воскресе́нїю твоемꙋ̀ Го́споди*)  
To the Theotokos:  
**"Most holy Theotokos, save us,"** and the rest.  
Only the Heirmoi of the first and of the last Canon are sung. At the end of each Ode, the Heirmos is sung, named the Katavasia of the cycle.  
Upon the completion of the Third Ode, the Little Litany is said, and the exclamation:  
**"For Thou art our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ ты̀ е҆сѝ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
Then the Kontakion and Sessional Hymn in the General Menaion of the Saint.

After the Sixth Ode, again the Little Litany and the exclamation:  
**"For Thou art the King of peace, and the Savior of our souls, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ты̀ бо е҆сѝ Ца́рь ми́ра...*)  
is said by the Priest. Then the Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos. (Its last verse is chanted in its tone).  
In the Eighth Ode there are four troparia; instead of "Glory...", we say:  
**"Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord."** (*Благослови́мъ Ѻ҆тца̀, и҆ Сы́на, и҆ свята́гѡ Дꙋ́ха, Го́спода*)  
— Before the Katavasia of the Eighth Ode we all make a metania together, saying:  
**"We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages."** (*Хва́лимъ, благослови́мъ, покланя́емсѧ Го́сподеви, пою́ще и҆ превознося́ще е҆го̀ во вся̀ вѣ́ки*)  
Then we sing the Heirmos of the Eighth Ode, the Katavasia (at the convergence).  
The Priest, having made a bow after the conclusion of the Eighth Ode, exclaims:  
**"The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!"** (*Богоро́дицꙋ, и҆ Ма́терь свѣ́та пѣ́снми возвели́чимъ*)  
And we, having gathered together, recite the Ninth Ode, singing with a loud voice:  
**"My soul doth magnify the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my Savior."** (*Вели́читъ дꙋша̀ моя̀ Го́спода...*)  
and:  
**"More honorable than the Cherubim, and more glorious beyond compare than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, true Theotokos, thee do we magnify."**  
We make also small bows.  
After the Ninth Ode the Little Litany is said, and the exclamation:  
**"For all the heavenly powers praise Thee, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ тя̀ хва́лѧтъ вся̀ си́лы небе́сныя...*)  
Then the Priest or the Deacon exclaims:  
**"Holy is the Lord our God,"** (*Свя́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*)  
in the occurring tone, three times. Verse 1:  
**"For holy is the Lord our God."**  
Verse 2:  
**"Over all people is our God."**  
— And after this, the Resurrectional Exapostilarion of the cycle, which is at the end of the Octoechos. Then: "Glory..." of the Saint, "Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," Resurrectional Theotokion.  
At the Praises the Choir sings:  
**"Let every breath praise the Lord. Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest: to Thee is due a hymn, O God."** (*Вся́кое дыха́нїе да хва́литъ Го́спода...*)  
And the second verse of Psalm 148 with this ending, in the occurring tone of the day; and they recite the remaining verses of Psalms 148, 149, and 150 unto the verse:  
**"To execute upon them the judgment written:"** (*Сотвори́ти въ ни́хъ сꙋ́дъ напи́санъ*) And we set eight verses. And we sing the Resurrectional and Anatolian Stichera written for the Praises, with refrains from the Psalter and two resurrectional ones: 1st,
**"Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, forget not Thy poor unto the end;"**  
and 2nd,  
**"I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, I will tell of all Thy marvelous works."**  
Both choirs also converge in the midst of the church.  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:"**  
The Gospel Sticheron of the cycle.  
**"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**  
Theotokion:  
**"Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos, for through Him Who became incarnate of thee Hades is taken captive, Adam is recalled, the curse is blotted out, Eve is set free, death is slain, and we are given life..."** (*Преблагослове́нна е҆сѝ Богоро́дице Дѣ́во...*)  
The Priest, having entered into the holy sanctuary, vests in the epitrachelion and phelonion, and having opened the holy doors, goes forth before the holy doors, and there makes one bow toward the holy table. And upon the completion of the chanting, the Priest, bowing, exclaims:  
**"Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light!"** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀ показа́вшемꙋ на́мъ свѣ́тъ*)  
The Choir with those present sings the Great Doxology. After this:  
**"Holy God..."** according to custom. Then the Resurrectional Troparion, one of the two:  
if it is Tone 1, 3, 5, or 7:  
**"Today salvation is come to the world: let us sing to Him Who rose from the tomb, the Author of our life; for having destroyed death by death, He hath given us the victory and great mercy."** (*Дне́сь спасенїе мі́рꙋ бы́сть...*)  
but if Tone 2, 4, 6, or 8, we sing this:  
**"Having risen from the tomb, and having burst the bonds of Hades, Thou hast destroyed the sentence of death, O Lord..."** (*Воскре́съ ѿ гро́ба...*)  
written in the Horologion. While this is sung, the Priest, having bowed once, enters into the sanctuary (and takes the Gospel Book from the Tetrapod, if it was placed there).  
Then the Priest, standing before the holy table, says the Litany:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy..."**  
and the exclamation:  
**"For Thou art a merciful God, and lovest mankind..."**  
After this:  
**"Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord."**  
And the exclamation:  
**"For Thou art a God of mercy, compassions, and love for mankind..."**  
> **Priest:** **"Peace be unto all."**
> **Choir:** **"And to thy spirit."**
> **Deacon:** **"Let us bow our heads unto the Lord."**
and after the second exclamation:  
**"For Thine it is to show mercy and to save us, O our God..."**  
the Priest immediately says:  
**"Wisdom!"** [*Standing in the royal doors facing the people*]  
> **Choir:** **"Bless!"**
> **Priest:** ""
**"Blessed and glorified is Christ our God, Who is, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**

**"always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Confirm, O God, the holy Catholic faith of Orthodox Christians, unto ages of ages!"** (*Оу҆твердѝ, Бо́же, ст҃ꙋю каѳоли́ческꙋ вѣ́рꙋ правосла́вныхъ хрїстїа́нъ во вѣ́къ вѣ́ка*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Most holy Theotokos, save us!"**  
> **Choir:** ""
**"More honorable than the Cherubim..."**  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!"**  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Lord, have mercy"** (three times). **"Bless!"**  
The Priest says the Dismissal:  
**"May Christ our true God, Who rose from the dead..."**  
and the rest, and commemorates the saint whose service is sung.  
After the Dismissal the royal doors are closed, and the Choir:  
**"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times),  
and reads the First Hour; and after the prayer:  
**"O Christ, the true Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that cometh into the world..."** (*Хрїстѐ свѣ́те и҆́стинный...*)  
the Priest says:  
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!"**  
and the Dismissal takes place.  
The Third and Sixth Hours are read by the Priest privately.

## The Order
### OF GREAT MATINS
#### On Weekdays, if there occurs a Saint having a Polyeleos.

The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, comes before the royal doors, and having made a bow there, says:  
**"Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
Then:  
**"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... Trisagion... Most Holy Trinity... Our Father..."**

and the rest of the customary beginning, and after  
**"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times),  
the Psalms of King David: Psalm 19: **"The Lord hear thee..."** (*Оу҆слы́шитъ тя̀ Го́сподь*) and Psalm 20: **"O Lord, in Thy strength..."** (*Го́споди си́лою твое́ю*).  
Then Trisagion, with "Our Father"; and after the exclamation, the Troparia:  
**"O Lord, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."**  
the Litany, the Six Psalms, and all the rest of Matins we chant as was prescribed above for Sunday on folio 30, unto the second Kathisma and its Sessional Hymn of the Saint.  
After the second Sessional Hymn with "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," and Theotokion, the Choir sings the Polyeleos, that is: verses from the 19th Kathisma:  
**"Praise the name of the Lord, praise the Lord, O ye servants..."** [Psalm 134]  
and:  
**"Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good: for His mercy endureth forever."** [Psalm 135]  
Then the Megalynarion of the Saint with selected verses from the Psalms. Thereupon the Little Litany is said; and the Choir sings the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," Theotokion.  
Then the Gradual Hymns (*Степе́нна* / *Anavathmoi*) of Tone 4, 1st Antiphon:  
**"From my youth up many passions war against me..."** (*Ѿ ю́ности моея̀ мно́зи бо́рютъ мя̀ стра́сти...*)  
[*4 verses, adding to the 3rd verse: "Glory...", and to the 4th: "Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."*]  
After these, the Priest, having put on also the phelonion, opens the holy doors and having exclaimed:  
**"Let us be attentive! Peace be unto all! Wisdom, let us be attentive!"**  
and all the rest, as is prescribed in Sunday Matins, reads in the sanctuary the Morning Gospel of the Saint.  
— After the Gospel, the Choir reads Psalm 50; the Priest goes forth with the Gospel Book and offers it for veneration.  
— The Choir:  
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: Through the prayers of [Name]"**  
[*that is: of the Archangel, or Apostle, or Prophet, or Martyr, etc., whose service is sung*]  
and:  
**"Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions."**  
Then the refrain:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out our iniquities."**  
and the Sticheron of the Saint. After this the Priest:
**"O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance;"**  
and the exclamation:  
**"Through the mercy and compassions, and love for mankind of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
and the rest, as was indicated a little above in Sunday Matins on folio 33, unto the end, except that at the Praises we set 4 verses, "Glory..." of the Saint, and "Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," Theotokion, and the rest.  
After the Great Doxology and both litanies, the Priest makes the Great Dismissal, as was indicated for Sunday; and after the Dismissal immediately the Choir:  
**"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times),  
and reads the First Hour; and after the Prayer:  
**"O Christ, the true Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that cometh into the world:"**  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!"**  
Then the complete Dismissal:  
**"May Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother..."**  

> **Remark.** If there is an All-Night Vigil for the Saint, then everything is sung as was prescribed above on folio 18. Whence it follows that for such a Matins the two preparatory Psalms, that is: 19 and 20, as also the Litany for the Emperor, are not read; but after the verse:  
> **"Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men"** (three times),  
> and after:  
> **"O Lord, Thou wilt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise,"**  
> the Six Psalms immediately follow, and everything else, as in the Matins of a Saint having a Polyeleos.[^48]

## The Order
### OF WEEKDAY VESPERS,
#### or Daily Vespers.

The Priest, having entered into the temple, makes three bows before the royal doors; he takes also the epitrachelion, and having blessed it and kissed the cross upon it, puts it upon himself; and standing before the royal doors, with a bow begins according to custom:  
**"Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."**
**"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... Trisagion... Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Most Holy Trinity... Lord, have mercy"** (three times). **"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**  
After "Our Father," the exclamation:  
**"For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** **"Amen. Lord, have mercy"** (12 times).
**"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times),  
and the Psalms of the Ninth Hour are read, and the Priest withdraws to his place.  
After its prayer:  
**"O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who art longsuffering..."** / **"O Master, abundant in mercy..."** (*Влады́ко многоми́лостиве*)  
the Choir immediately:  
**"Come, let us worship and bow down..."** (three times),  
and the Vesperal Psalm 103:  
**"Bless the Lord, O my soul: O Lord my God, Thou art become exceedingly great."** (*Благословѝ дꙋшѐ моя̀ Го́спода, Го́споди Бо́же мо́й...*)  
[*If at any time the Ninth Hour is not sung together with Vespers, but previously with the other Hours, then Vespers begins from the customary beginning*].  
— While the Introductory Psalm is sung, the Priest according to the Typikon, having stood before the holy doors, says the Vesperal Prayers (of Light).  
After "Glory... Now and forever, and unto the ages of ages," and "Alleluia" (three times), the Priest before the holy doors says the Litany...

Great Litany: **"In peace let us pray to the Lord."** (*Ми́ромъ Го́сподꙋ помо́лимсѧ*)  
And after its exclamation, the Kathisma of the cycle is read (except on Sunday evening, on which Kathisma is not said at Vespers throughout the entire year, but only on weekdays).[^49]  
After the Kathisma, the Little Litany is said by the Priest, which the Choir sings in the customary manner.  
After this we sing: **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), that is, the verse:  
**"Lord, I have cried unto Thee, hear Thou me; hear Thou me, O Lord. Lord, I have cried unto Thee, hear Thou me; attend to the voice of my supplication, when I cry unto Thee, hear Thou me, O Lord."** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ къ тебѣ̀ оу҆слы́ши мѧ̀...*)  
And the 2nd verse:  
**"Let my prayer be set forth as incense before Thee; the lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice: hear Thou me, O Lord."** (*Да и҆спра́витсѧ моли́тва моѧ̀... съ ѡ҆конча́нїемъ: оу҆слы́ши мѧ̀, Го́споди*)  
The remaining verses of the psalm are read.  
Then Psalm 141: **"I cried unto the Lord with my voice..."** (*Гла́сомъ мои́мъ ко Го́сподꙋ воззва́хъ*) and Psalm 129: **"Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord..."** (*И҆зъ глꙋбины̀ воззва́хъ къ тебѣ̀, Го́споди*) down to the verse: **"If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand?"** (*А҆́ще беззако́нїѧ на́зриши, Го́споди, Го́споди, кто̀ постои́тъ*).  
And we set 6 stichera, and we chant them to the stichera on **"Lord, I have cried"**, from the Octoechos and of the Saint. [Concerning the rest of the stichera, to whom they belong, see the declaration below in the rubrics].  
Then upon the conclusion of the stichera, when the Choir sings: **"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen:"** Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, the Priest, having come before the holy doors (in the epitrachelion), says:  
**"Wisdom! Standing upright."** (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти*)  
The Choir sings:  
**"Gladsome Light"** (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Let us be attentive. Peace be unto all. Wisdom, let us be attentive."** (*Во́нмимъ: Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ, Премꙋ́дрость во́нмимъ*)  
And the Choir sings the Prokeimenon of the day, which is found in the Horologion.  
The Reader reads its verse, and again the Prokeimenon.  
After this is immediately read:  
**"Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin..."** (*Сподо́би, Го́споди, въ ве́черъ се́й...*)  
Then the Priest says before the holy doors the litany:  
**"Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord."** (*И҆спо́лнимъ вече́рнюю моли́твꙋ на́шꙋ Го́сподеви*)  
And after its exclamation:
**"Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Бꙋ́ди держа́ва ца́рствїа твоегѡ̀...*)  
The Priest having departed, the Choir sings the Stichera at the Aposticha (*Стихи̑ры на стихо́внѣхъ*); then reads:  
**"Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word..."** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*)  
Trisagion: **"Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us"** (thrice). **"Most Holy Trinity, have mercy on us..."** **"Our Father..."**  
The Priest exclaims:  
**"For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ твоѐ е́сть ца́рство...*)  
> **Choir:** **"Amen,"** and sings the Troparion of the Saint whose commemoration falls on the following day.
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen:"** Theotokion.  
And immediately the Litany by the Priest:  
**"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee, hear and have mercy."** (*Поми́лꙋй на́съ, Бо́же, по вели́цѣй ми́лости твое́й...*)  
And after its exclamation, the Priest says:  
**"Wisdom!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, the very Theotokos, thee do we magnify."** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю херꙋві́мъ...*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee."** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀, Хрⷭ҇тѐ Бо́же, оу҆пова́нїе на́ше, сла́ва тебѣ̀*)  
> **Choir:** ""
**"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Lord, have mercy"** (thrice). **"Bless, Father."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ: Го́споди поми́лꙋй, три́жды. Благословѝ*)  
> **Priest:** ""
**"Christ our true God..."** (*Хрⷭ҇то́съ и҆́стинный Бо́гъ на́шъ...*)  
and the rest of the daily Dismissal, in which he commemorates also the Saint whose service is chanted.  
**Behold.** In this manner Vespers is chanted on Sunday evening, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday, if on the morrow there is a commemoration of only one or two minor saints.  
If, however, you read Vespers privately (*на еди́нѣ*), you have for this the general service for every day to our Lord Jesus Christ in the Horologion: In the evening at **"Lord, I have cried"**, stichera, Tone 6: **"When Thou shalt come to execute righteous judgment..."** (*Е҆гда̀ хо́щеши прїитѝ, сꙋ́дъ пра́ведный сотвори́ти*), and the rest. Likewise also at the Aposticha.[^50]  
* * *  
**Note.** Be it known that on weekdays following the dismissal of Vespers and Matins, there is sometimes a procession into the narthex, or a Litiya for the reposed.

When this takes place, the Priest, having taken the censer, goes forth into the narthex or into the midst of the church, two candle-bearers with lamps preceding him. The Choir follows, and singing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the Saint to whom the temple is dedicated, goes forth likewise into the church narthex or into the midst of the church. And after the sticheron, the Priest, having placed the epitrachelion upon himself, says the beginning:[^51]  
**"Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**  
> **Choir:** **"Amen."** Trisagion, and after **"Our Father..."**: the exclamation: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."**
Thereupon are sung the troparia:
